Q/K HEAD-NORM REFERENCE, MEASUREMENT ONLY
Captured 2026-10-01 at repo commit 5a63ab3 (probe tools/s0_qknorm_probe.cpp,
run atomically via tools/run_s0_probe.sh so a stale binary can never be run).
Model /var/lib/spoon/models/qwen3-8b/Qwen3-8B-Q4_K_M.gguf, token 785 "The",
position 7, layer 0. Read from llama.cpp's own graph through
ggml_backend_sched_eval_callback; nothing here reimplements attention.

STATUS: Q HEAD SEALED. K HEAD PARTIALLY SEALED -- pre-norm NOT captured.
The BAD=1 below is that missing K pre-norm and is deliberately left failing
rather than relaxed into green. Weakening the threshold to 5e-2 was tried and
reverted; a gate weakened until it passes is not a gate.

WHY SNAPSHOTTING IS REQUIRED, not merely convenient. ggml runs the head norm,
and RoPE, IN PLACE. Pointer equality proves an edge exists -- roped.src0 ==
normed.ptr -- but retaining a backend pointer and reading it after the callback
returns yields whatever an in-place successor wrote, not what was there. So
every tensor is copied into probe-owned storage at its own eval callback, and
the pointer is retained separately as provenance only. Both are needed: the
pointer is graph evidence, the copy is the reading.

CORRELATION IS BY GRAPH IDENTITY. A normed/roped pair is accepted only when
roped.src0 == normed.data. Name matching cannot work here: the inventory over
1266 events is Qcur x180, Kcur x144, Qcur_normed x36, Kcur_normed x36 -- these
names are per-layer and never unique. First-match-by-name paired one layer with
another and produced a post-norm vector spanning +-12.2 beside a post-RoPE
vector spanning +-0.27, which cannot be one vector because a rotation preserves
magnitude.

PAIRING IS DETERMINED EMPIRICALLY. Which elements RoPE rotates is a property of
the model, not of RoPE. Adjacent (k, k+1) GPT pairing gives worst-case relative
pairwise L2 error 2.65 for Q and 2.55 for K: decisively NOT preserved. NEOX
(k, k+64) pairing gives 7.72e-08 and 8.69e-08: preserved to float precision.
Qwen3 uses NEOX. An adjacent-pair gate would have FAILED a correct model, which
is the same error class as a gate that passes a wrong one.

STILL STANDING FROM 2e78f9c, PROVED FROM SOURCE: the head norm is applied BEFORE
RoPE for both Q and K (qwen3.cpp line 88 norm vs 91 rope; 97 norm vs 100 rope).
It is RMS, not LayerNorm: LLM_NORM_RMS maps to ggml_rms_norm at
llama-graph.cpp:1600, and qwen3.cpp never creates the bias tensors.
epsilon 9.999999974752427e-07, key_length 128, head_count 32, head_count_kv 8.

NOT YET RESOLVED, and it is a capture gap not a product decision: the K
projection's output was never snapshotted, so K's pre-RMSNorm vector is unknown.
Every earlier event sharing the K normed address was offered as a candidate and
RMSNorm's own identity out = in * weight * inv_rms selected the best, which
still errs by 0.0102 -- so the true K projection output is on some address this
run did not capture. Storage reuse across attn_norm, the projections and the head
norms is the cause. Q does not have this problem: its candidate reconstructs to
0.0013, which is float noise against values spanning +-12.

DETERMINISM: a second clean run reproduced identical hashes for all six
snapshots.
